<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed inverse temperature $`0\lt \beta\lt 1`$, we prove that the unscaled spectral gap of single-site heat-bath dynamics for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model is bounded away from zero with probability tending to one over the disorder. Equivalently, the Gibbs law satisfies a dimension-free Poincaré inequality for all functions.

- **claim-main-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a finite constant $C_\beta$, depending only on $\beta$, such that $$\mathbb P_J\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mu_0}(f)\le C_\beta\mathcal D_0(f) \text{ for every }f:\{-1,1\}^n\longrightarrow\mathbb R\right\} \longrightarrow1.$$ Consequently, the discrete-time heat-bath chain that chooses a uniform site has spectral gap at least $1/(C_\beta n)$ with probability tending to one.
  - Explanation: For each fixed inverse temperature below one, a variance-versus-Dirichlet-form bound holds for all functions with probability tending to one over the disorder, so the uniform-site heat-bath chain has a gap of order one over the dimension.
- **claim-simultaneous-words** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-words)): Fix $L\in\mathbb N$, $M_0<\infty$, and $c>0$. There are constants $C,c_1>0$ and $n_0$, depending only on $j,a_+,L,M_0,c$, such that the following event has probability at least $1-Ce^{-c_1n}$ for $n\ge n_0$. Simultaneously for all words $Q$ of length at most $L$, all their real diagonal factors of norm at most $M_0$, and all $0\le A\le a_+I$, one has $$\label{eq:word-estimates} \lVert Q\rVert\le C,\qquad \left(\sum_{a\ne b}\|Q_{ab}\|^4\right)^{1/4}\le C,\qquad \lVert \mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(Q-\mathsf P(Q))\rVert_2\le C.$$ For a word containing $K_A(J)$, the assertion is restricted to parameters satisfying $S_A(1;J)\succeq cI$. The same conclusion holds with $W$ in place of $J$, with the corresponding stability restriction. Thus all diagonal parameters in this assertion may be chosen after the random matrix has been observed.* *If a word has $r$ ordinary occurrences of $M$ outside its one $K_A(z;M)$ factor, then $\mathsf P(Q;z)$ has degree at most $r$.
  - Explanation: With overwhelming probability, products of bounded length built from the random matrix and arbitrary bounded diagonals are controlled simultaneously in operator norm, off-diagonal fourth norm and diagonal deviation from the noncrossing prediction.
- **claim-matrix-path-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-matrix-path-stability)): There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $j,a_+$, with the following property. For every fixed deterministic $0\le A\le a_+I$, and every fixed $R>2\sqrt j$, there are constants $C,c_2>0$ and $n_0$, uniform in $A$, such that $$\label{eq:deterministic-path-stability} \mathbb P\left\{\lVert W\rVert\le R\ \hbox{and}\quad S_A(z;W)\succeq c_0I\text{ for all }0\le z\le1\right\} \ge1-Ce^{-c_2n}\qquad(n\ge n_0).$$ In addition, for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, $\mathbb P\{\lVert W\rVert>2\sqrt j+\varepsilon\}\le C_\varepsilon e^{-c_\varepsilon n}$ for sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: For every fixed deterministic diagonal, the symmetric matrix along the interpolation path keeps a positive lower margin with exponentially high probability, and the operator norm stays near the spectral edge.
- **claim-word-bias** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-word-bias)): For the truncated words just defined, uniformly in deterministic diagonal parameters in the indicated bounded ranges, in $z\in[0,1]$, and in the diagonal index $i$, $$\label{eq:word-bias} \mathbb E\widehat Q_\theta(z)_{ii} =\mathsf P(Q_\theta;z)_{ii}+O(n^{-1}).$$ For words without $K_A$, the prediction has no $z$ dependence. The formal prediction for a word with $r$ ordinary $W$ letters is a polynomial of degree at most $r$.
  - Explanation: Expected diagonal entries of truncated words match the formal noncrossing prediction up to an error of reciprocal order in the dimension, uniformly in the bounded parameters.
- **claim-bilinear-equivalents** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bilinear)): Fix an integer $m\ge1$, a vector bound $M_1<\infty$, a tolerance $\varepsilon>0$, and $R>2\sqrt j$. For every deterministic $0\le A\le a_+I$ and deterministic vectors $v_1,\ldots,v_m\in\mathbb R^n$ with $\lVert v_r\rVert\le M_1$, there is an event of probability at least $1-Ce^{-c_3n}$ on which <a href="#eq:deterministic-path-stability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:deterministic-path-stability">[eq:deterministic-path-stability]</a> holds and, with $$G_A=A^{1/2}S_A(1;W)^{-1}A^{1/2}=K_A(W)A,$$ one has, for every $r,s$, $$\begin{aligned} \|v_r^T(G_A-A)v_s\|&\le\varepsilon, \label{eq:bilinear-G}\\ \|v_r^T(G_AW-B_AA)v_s\|&\le\varepsilon, \label{eq:bilinear-GW}\\ \|v_r^T(WG_AW-B_AI-B_A^2A)v_s\|&\le\varepsilon. \label{eq:bilinear-WGW} \end{aligned}$$ Here $C,c_3,n_0$ may depend on $j,a_+,m,M_1,\varepsilon,R$ but are uniform in $A$, the vectors, and $n\ge n_0$. The assertion is for each deterministic choice of these parameters; it does not claim simultaneity over all vector choices.
  - Explanation: For a prescribed finite family of deterministic vectors, three bilinear forms of the resolvent-type matrix concentrate around deterministic predictions with exponentially small failure probability; no simultaneity over vector choices is claimed.
- **claim-logdet-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-logdet)): Fix $\varepsilon>0$, an integer $r_0\ge0$, and $M_2<\infty$. There is $\gamma_0>0$, depending only on $j,\varepsilon$, such that for every fixed $0<\gamma\le\gamma_0$ and all sufficiently large $n$, an event of probability at least $1-Ce^{-c_4n}$ has the following property. Simultaneously for all diagonal $0\le V\le I$ and all real matrices $E$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}E\le r_0$ and $\lVert E\rVert\le M_2$, put $$b=\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}V,\qquad B=jb,\qquad L=I+(BI-W)V+E.$$ Then $$\label{eq:logdet-upper-bound} \frac1n\log\det(L^TL+\gamma I)\le jb^2+\varepsilon.$$ The constants $C,c_4$ and the lower threshold on $n$ may depend on $j,\varepsilon,\gamma,r_0,M_2$. In particular, $V$ and $E$ may depend on $W$.
  - Explanation: A regularized normalized log-determinant is bounded above uniformly over all diagonal matrices and bounded finite-rank perturbations, even when these are chosen after observing the matrix.
- **claim-scalar-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-entropy)): Let $0<j<1$, $d\in\mathbb R$, and $s>0$. Let $P$ be a probability law on $\mathbb R$ with finite second moment, and set $$m(y)=\tanh y,\qquad q=\mathbb E_P m^2,\qquad b=1-q,\qquad a=\mathbb E_P[(y-d)m],\qquad S=s+jq.$$ If $s\ge jq$, then $$D(P\Vert N(d,s))\ge \frac{j(a-sb)^2}{2sS}. \label{eq:scalar-entropy}$$ Equality holds if and only if $P=N(d,s)$.
  - Explanation: Relative entropy to a Gaussian law is bounded below by a squared discrepancy between the field-tangent pairing and the Onsager-type target, with equality only for the Gaussian law.
- **claim-gaussian-equality** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-equality)): Fix $0<j<1$. For each $t\ge0$, the equation $$s_*(t)=t+j\mathbb E_{N(s_*(t),s_*(t))}\tanh y \label{eq:scalar-fixed-point}$$ has a unique nonnegative solution, where $N(0,0)$ means the point mass at zero. Its associated Gaussian law $P_*(t)=N(s_*(t),s_*(t))$ is the unique Gaussian law satisfying <a href="#eq:scalar-consistency" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-consistency">[eq:scalar-consistency]</a> with $\sigma=0$. Thus, among consistent laws with $s>0$, equality in <a href="#eq:scalar-entropy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-entropy">[eq:scalar-entropy]</a> holds precisely at $P_*(t)$.* *The function $t\mapsto s_*(t)$ is Lipschitz with constant $(1-j)^{-1}$, and $$t\le s_*(t)\le\frac{t}{1-j},\qquad q_*(t):=\mathbb E_{P_*(t)}\tanh^2 y =\frac{s_*(t)-t}{j}\le\frac{t}{1-j}. \label{eq:scalar-fixed-point-bounds}$$ In particular $s_*(0)=q_*(0)=0$, and $s_*(t),q_*(t)>0$ for $t>0$.
  - Explanation: The scalar consistency equations have a unique Gaussian solution whose mean equals its variance; its variance is Lipschitz in time and bounded by a multiple of time.
- **claim-stable-fields** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-stable-fields)): Fix $j\in(0,1)$ and $0<T<\infty$. There are constants $\eta,c,\epsilon_A,\rho_0,a>0$, depending only on $j,T$, and events $\mathcal J_n$ for the zero-diagonal Gaussian matrix $J$, such that $\mathbb P(J\in\mathcal J_n)\longrightarrow1$ and the following assertion holds for every $J\in\mathcal J_n$. Let $X\sim\mu_0$, let $B_0$ be an independent standard $n$-dimensional Brownian motion, and put $Y(t)=tX+B_0(t)$. With conditional probability at least $1-e^{-an}$, simultaneously for $0\le t\le T$, $y\in\mathbb R^n$, and all diagonal $A$, $$\left. \begin{gathered} \lVert F_{J,Y(t)}(y)\rVert\le2\rho_0\sqrt n,\\ 0\le A\le(1+\eta)I,\qquad \lVert A-V_y\rVert_{\mathrm F}\le\epsilon_A\sqrt n \end{gathered} \right\} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathsf H_J(y,A)\succ cI. \label{eq:stable-field}$$ We may require $j(1+\eta)^2<1$ and $\lVert J\rVert\le2\sqrt j+\theta$ on $\mathcal J_n$, for any sufficiently small fixed $\theta>0$ chosen in the proof.
  - Explanation: Along the Gaussian observation path, with high conditional probability, every approximate solution of the field equation with a nearby diagonal has a Hessian proxy bounded below by a positive constant.
- **claim-stopping-time** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-stopping)): For every $J\in\mathcal J_n$ there is an observation stopping time $\tau_0$ such that $$\mathbb P(\tau_0\le T\mid J)\le e^{-an},$$ and, for every $t\in[0,T]$ with $t<\tau_0$, the implication <a href="#eq:stable-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-field">[eq:stable-field]</a> holds at $Y(t)$ with residual threshold $\rho_0\sqrt n$ in place of $2\rho_0\sqrt n$. The stopping rule depends on $J$ and the observations, and is independent of any test function.
  - Explanation: Stability along observations can be expressed through a stopping time, independent of any test function, that is reached before the horizon only with exponentially small probability.
- **claim-gaussian-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-reduction)): With the preceding definitions, and with $g'$ an independent standard Gaussian vector, $$\mathbb EI_{t,\sigma}(\mathcal E) =\int \sqrt{\frac sS}\,G_{t,\sigma,n}(y) \mathbb P\left((y,\widetilde J)\in\mathcal E\,\middle\|\, Wm+\sqrt{t+\sigma^2}\,g'=z\right)\,\mathrm dy. \label{eq:conditional-integral}$$ The conditional distribution in this identity is the usual Gaussian conditional distribution and is defined for every $y$, since $\sigma>0$.
  - Explanation: The expected restricted field integral under the planted law reduces to an integral of the scalar Gaussian-type density times a conditional probability given the effective observation.
- **claim-scalar-rates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-rates)): Fix $j\in(0,1)$ and $T<\infty$.* 1. *For every $s_{\min}>0$ and $L<\infty$, one can choose $R<\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over $0\le t\le T$ and $0<\sigma\le1$, $$\int_{\{s\ge s_{\min},\,\langle y^2\rangle>R^2\}} G_{t,\sigma,n}(y)\,\mathrm dy\le e^{-Ln}.$$ For every $\varepsilon>0$, the unrestricted integral over $\{s\ge s_{\min}\}$ is at most $e^{\varepsilon n}$ for all sufficiently large $n$.* 2. *Fix $t_0>0$. Outside any prescribed open neighborhood of the equality curve $\{(P_t,t):t_0\le t\le T\}$, there are $a,\sigma_*>0$ such that the integral of $G_{t,\sigma,n}$ is at most $e^{-an}$, uniformly over $t_0\le t\le T$ and $0<\sigma\le\sigma_*$, for all sufficiently large $n$. The neighborhood can impose weak closeness of the empirical law and closeness of any finite list of continuous, at most linearly growing averages, including all parameters in <a href="#eq:scalar-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scalar-parameters">[eq:scalar-parameters]</a>.* 3. *For every $q_0>0$, there are $t_0,\sigma_*,a>0$ such that $$\int_{\{q\ge q_0\}}G_{t,\sigma,n}(y)\,\mathrm dy\le e^{-an}$$ uniformly over $0\le t\le t_0$ and $0<\sigma\le\sigma_*$, for all sufficiently large $n$.* 4. *Fix $t_0>0$ and $\nu>0$. For all sufficiently large $R_1$ there is $a>0$ such that $$\int_{\{\langle y^2\mathbf 1_{\{\|y\|>R_1\}}\rangle>\nu\}} G_{t,\sigma,n}(y)\,\mathrm dy\le e^{-an},$$ uniformly over $t_0\le t\le T$ and $0<\sigma\le1$, for all sufficiently large $n$.* *All constants are independent of $n,t,\sigma$ in the indicated ranges. In <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, neighborhoods are interpreted on each fixed second-moment ball; the complement of a sufficiently large such ball is covered by <span class="upright">(i)</span>.
  - Explanation: The scalar density integral has uniform exponential control: tail bounds, strictly negative rates away from the equality curve, small-overlap control near time zero, and control of large coordinates.
- **claim-conditional-field-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-field-stability)): Fix $0<t_0\le T$, $R<\infty$, and $\eta>0$ with $j(1+\eta)^2<1$. There are $c_*,\epsilon_*,a_*,\sigma_*>0$, a neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $\{(P_t,t):t_0\le t\le T\}$, a tail tolerance $\nu>0$, and a finite $R_1$ such that the following holds. Suppose $$\langle y^2\rangle\le R^2,\qquad (P_y,t)\in\mathcal U,\qquad \langle y^2\mathbf 1_{\{\|y\|>R_1\}}\rangle\le\nu, \qquad t_0\le t\le T,\quad0<\sigma\le\sigma_*.$$ Under the Gaussian conditioning in <a href="#eq:conditional-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditional-integral">[eq:conditional-integral]</a>, with probability at least $1-e^{-a_*n}$, simultaneously for all diagonal $A$ satisfying $$0\le A\le(1+\eta)I,\qquad \lVert A-V_y\rVert_{\mathrm F}\le\epsilon_*\sqrt n,$$ one has $\mathsf H_{\widetilde J}(y,A)\succeq c_*I$. All constants are uniform over the displayed ranges and sufficiently large $n$. The tail cutoff $R_1$ may be required to be arbitrarily large after choosing $\nu$; $\epsilon_*$ is then decreased if necessary.
  - Explanation: Near the scalar equality curve, under the Gaussian conditioning, the Hessian proxy stays bounded below simultaneously for all nearby diagonals with exponentially high probability.
- **claim-local-field-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-field-volume)): Fix $j,K<\infty$, $r>0$, and $\varepsilon>0$. Suppose $\lVert \widetilde J\rVert\le K$, and a set $E\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ contains the ball $\{y':\lVert y'-y\rVert\le r\sqrt n\}$. Put $$L=F'_{\widetilde J,h_0}(y) =I+(BI-\widetilde J)V_y-\frac{2j}{n}mm^{\mathsf T}V_y,\qquad Q=L^{\mathsf T}L+\gamma I.$$ If $$\frac1n\log\det Q\le jb(y)^2+\varepsilon_1,\qquad \lVert F_{\widetilde J,h_0}(y)\rVert\le\rho\sqrt n, \label{eq:local-volume-hypotheses}$$ then $$(2\pi\sigma^2)^{-n/2}\int_E e^{-\lVert F_{\widetilde J,h_0}(y')\rVert^2/(2\sigma^2)+njb(y')^2/2}\,\mathrm dy' \ge e^{-\varepsilon n} \label{eq:local-volume-conclusion}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, provided the constants are chosen in this order: first $\varepsilon_1>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $\varepsilon$; then any fixed $\gamma>0$; then $\sigma>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $j,K,r,\varepsilon,\gamma$; and finally $\rho>0$ sufficiently small in terms of those constants and $\sigma$. The bound is uniform in $y,h_0,n$ under the stated hypotheses.
  - Explanation: An approximate zero of the field map satisfying a regularized determinant bound forces the Gaussian-weighted field integral over a surrounding ball to be at least exponentially small in the dimension, with constants chosen in a stated order.
- **claim-root-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-root)): Fix $j,K,c,r_0>0$. Let $J$ be symmetric with $\lVert J\rVert\le K$, and let $h_0\in\mathbb R^n$. Suppose $$\mathsf H_J(y,V_y)\succeq cI \quad\text{whenever}\quad \lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\rVert\le r_0\sqrt n. \label{eq:root-stability-assumption}$$ Then $F_{J,h_0}$ has exactly one zero $r\in\mathbb R^n$, and $$\lVert y-r\rVert\le C\lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\rVert \quad\text{if}\quad\lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\rVert<r_0\sqrt n, \qquad C=1+(K+3j)/c. \label{eq:root-distance}$$ In particular this conclusion applies to every field satisfying <a href="#eq:stable-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stable-field">[eq:stable-field]</a>, even if that implication is required only through residual threshold $\rho_0\sqrt n$.
  - Explanation: If the Hessian proxy is positive on the whole small-residual region, the field equation has exactly one zero, and the distance to it is linearly controlled by the residual.
- **claim-primary-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-primary-derivatives)): Let $$H_1=J,\qquad M_0=I,\qquad M_l=D_{1-(m^l)^2}H_l,\qquad H_{l+1}=JM_l-jb_lM_{l-1}. \label{eq:primary-formal}$$ For each fixed $l\geq1$, $$\begin{aligned} &\lVert \nabla h^l-H_l\rVert_{\mathrm F} +\lVert \nabla m^l-M_l\rVert_{\mathrm F}\leq C,\label{eq:primary-error}\\ &\lVert \nabla h^l\rVert+\lVert \nabla m^l\rVert +\lVert \nabla h^l\rVert_{\ell^4} +\lVert \mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\nabla h^l\rVert_2\leq C, \qquad \lVert db_l\rVert\leq Cn^{-1/2}. \label{eq:primary-bounds} \end{aligned}$$ More generally, let $g_i$ be a smooth function of a fixed list of coordinates $h_i^l$ and averages $b_l$, with uniformly bounded derivatives through the orders being used. Deterministic site labels are permitted, provided the same bounds hold uniformly in $i$. Its derivative matrix has bounded operator norm, bounded diagonal $\ell^2$ norm, and bounded entrywise $\ell^4$ norm.
  - Explanation: Discrete derivatives of the primary Onsager-corrected iterates agree with formal matrix chains up to a bounded Frobenius error and obey operator, entrywise fourth and diagonal bounds.
- **claim-implicit-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-implicit-construction)): For sufficiently small $\rho>0$, <a href="#eq:implicit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:implicit">[eq:implicit]</a> has a unique solution on $\Omega_{2\rho}$. There are constants independent of the primary depth $k$ such that, throughout this region, $$\lVert w\rVert+\lVert y^{\rm imp}\rVert+\sqrt n\|c_1\|\leq C, \qquad I+j\chi A-A^{1/2}JA^{1/2}\succeq cI. \label{eq:implicit-size}$$ On $\Omega_{3\rho/2}$, for all sufficiently large $n$ depending on the fixed depth, the solution also satisfies $$\lVert \nabla w\rVert_{\mathrm F}+\lVert \nabla y^{\rm imp}\rVert_{\mathrm F} +\sqrt n\lVert dc_1\rVert\leq C_k. \label{eq:implicit-frobenius}$$
  - Explanation: The implicit auxiliary field is uniquely solvable in a small-residual region with size bounds and a positive inverse margin independent of depth, and has bounded Frobenius derivatives on a smaller region.
- **claim-small-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-derivatives)): Every source, auxiliary field, and terminal vector $U$ in a fixed ordinary admissible recipe satisfies $$\lVert U\rVert+\lVert \nabla U\rVert_{\mathrm F} +\lVert \mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\nabla U\rVert_1\leq C. \label{eq:small-derivative-bound}$$ The same holds locally for recipes with the single initial implicit field above. In these recipes the initial partial $\partial_k w$ is also a small source. For every primary coefficient and auxiliary coefficient in <a href="#eq:small-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-field">[eq:small-field]</a>, respectively, $$\begin{split} \alpha_l=\langle \partial_lw^a\rangle:&\quad \|\alpha_l\|+\lVert d\alpha_l\rVert\leq C/\sqrt n,\\ \gamma_b=\langle \partial_{y^b}w^a\rangle:&\quad \|\gamma_b\|+\lVert d\gamma_b\rVert\leq C. \end{split} \label{eq:small-average-bounds}$$ All local statements include the required fixed flip buffers. Constants can depend on the entire finite recipe and its primary depth.
  - Explanation: Every vector built by an ordinary admissible recipe has bounded norm, bounded Frobenius derivative and bounded diagonal derivative sum, because formal trace predictions cancel exactly.
- **claim-weak-residual** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-residual)): Let $U$ be a terminal vector in a fixed recipe admissible from level $p\geq1$. For an ordinary recipe and every scalar $G$, $$\left\|\mathbb EG R_p^{\mathsf T}U\right\|\leq C\lVert G\rVert_*. \label{eq:weak-residual}$$ For a local recipe with implicit starting index $k$, the same conclusion holds when $p\leq k$ and $G$ is supported on $\Omega_\rho$.* *There is also an ordinary square-density version. If $\sup_x(\|H_0(x)\|+\lVert dH_0(x)\rVert)\leq C_0$, then for every $f$, $$\left\|\mathbb Ef^2H_0 R_p^{\mathsf T}U\right\| \leq C\{\mathbb Ef^2+\mathcal D_{h_0}(f)\}. \label{eq:square-residual}$$ Constants are uniform in $f,G,H_0$ subject to the stated bound, and may depend on $p$, the recipe, its coefficient bounds, and the finite set of word diagnostics needed in the proof.
  - Explanation: Residual pairings against admissible terminal vectors are bounded by the dual norm of the test function, in an ordinary version, a local version and a square-density version.
- **claim-terminal-residual** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-residual)): For the implicit construction at index $k$ and every $G$ supported on $\Omega_\rho$, $$\left\|\mathbb EG\left[ \{h^k-h_0-(J-jb_{k-1}I)m^k\}^{\mathsf T}w -j(b_k-b_{k-1})n c_1\right]\right\| \leq C\lVert G\rVert_*. \label{eq:terminal-residual}$$
  - Explanation: The pairing of the implicit source with the matrix residual, corrected by an averaged term, is bounded by the dual norm of test functions supported on the small-residual region.
- **claim-residual-selection** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-selection)): For each sufficiently small fixed $\rho>0$, there is a fixed depth $d$ and functions $C_k$, $2\le k\le d$, with the following properties:* 1. *$0\le C_k\le1$, and $C_k$ is supported where $\max(\lVert R_k\rVert,\lVert R_{k-1}\rVert)\le\rho\sqrt n$. It equals one where both norms are at most $\rho\sqrt n/2$, and $\lVert dC_k\rVert\le C_\rho/\sqrt n$ pointwise.* 2. *Set $\widehat C_k=C_k\prod_{i=2}^{k-1}(1-C_i)$ and $D=1-\sum_{k=2}^d\widehat C_k$. Then $0\le D\le1$ and $$\mathbb P_{\mu_{h_0}}(D>0)\le\frac{C_\rho}{n}.$$ For $N=\mathbb Ef^2>0$ and $\nu=f^2\mu_{h_0}/N$, $$\mathbb P_\nu(D>0)\le\frac{C_\rho}{\sqrt n} \left(1+\frac{\mathcal D_{h_0}(f)}{N}\right).$$* *All constants are uniform over the field and matrix under the stated diagnostics. The depth and the required diagnostic length may depend on $\rho$.
  - Explanation: Smooth cutoffs select regions where two consecutive residuals are small, leaving a deficit set of small probability under the Gibbs law and under square reweighting.
- **claim-directional-covariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-directional)): There is a constant $C$ such that, at every field satisfying the above stable-field implication, $$\label{eq:directional} \lVert \mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}_{\mu_{h_0}}(G,x)\rVert \le C\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mu_{h_0}}(G)+\mathcal D_{h_0}(G)\bigr)^{1/2}$$ for every $G$. The constant is uniform in $n,h_0$, and $G$ on the specified matrix events; only a fixed finite diagnostic length is required.
  - Explanation: The Euclidean norm of the covariance of any function with the spin vector is controlled by the square root of its variance plus Dirichlet form at every stable field.
- **claim-weighted-mean** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-mean)): For every sufficiently small fixed $\delta>0$, there is a constant $C_\delta$ such that, for $N=\mathbb E_{\mu_{h_0}}f^2>0$ and $\nu=f^2\mu_{h_0}/N$, $$\label{eq:weighted-mean} \frac{\lVert \mathbb E_\nu x-t_*\rVert}{\sqrt n} \le C\delta+\frac{C_\delta}{\sqrt n} \left(1+\frac{\mathcal D_{h_0}(f)}{N}\right).$$ The leading constant $C$ is independent of $\delta$ and of the selection depth. This estimate requires only ordinary word diagnostics, of a fixed length that may depend on $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Under any square-density reweighting, the normalized distance between the reweighted mean and the deterministic root is bounded by a small tolerance plus a term vanishing like the inverse square root of the dimension.
- **claim-signed-curvature** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-curvature)): There are universal constants $u_0>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for every $n$, every symmetric zero-diagonal $J$ satisfying $\max_{i,j}\|U_{ij}\|\le u_0$, every $h_0\in\mathbb R^n$, and every real function $f$ on the discrete cube, $$\mathbb E_{\mu_{h_0}}[(Hf)^2] \ge (1-Cr_4(U))\mathcal D_{h_0}(f) -\mathbb E_{\mu_{h_0}}\bigl[a^{\mathsf T}Ua +C\|a\|^{\mathsf T}Q\|a\|\bigr]. \label{eq:terminal-curvature}$$ Here $\|a\|$ denotes coordinatewise absolute value.
  - Explanation: An exact two-spin calculation bounds the second moment of the generator below by the Dirichlet form minus a signed quadratic cost, an entrywise-square cost and a quartic row error.
- **claim-terminal-matrices** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-matrices)): Fix $j\in(0,1)$. There is a finite constant $M$, depending only on $j$, with the following property. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\alpha\in(0,1/2)$ such that, with probability tending to one over $J$, $$\begin{gathered} \max_{i,j}\|J_{ij}\|\le n^{-1/4},\qquad \\|J\\|,\\|U\\|,\\|Q\\|\le M,\qquad r_4(U)\le Mn^{-1/2}, \label{eq:terminal-global-matrix}\displaybreak[1]\\ \\|U_{SS}\\|\le\varepsilon,\qquad \\|Q_{SS}\\|\le\varepsilon \quad\text{for every }S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\} \text{ with }\|S\|\le\alpha n. \label{eq:terminal-sparse-matrix} \end{gathered}$$ The same $M$ works for all $\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: With probability tending to one, the interaction matrix has bounded norms and small principal submatrices on all coordinate sets of proportional size.
- **claim-terminal-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-gap)): Fix $j\in(0,1)$. There are finite $T>0$, $c>0$, and events $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm{term}}$ depending only on $J$, with $\mathbb P_J(\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm{term}})\to1$, such that the following holds for every sufficiently large $n$ and every $J\in\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm{term}}$. Let $X$ have any probability law on $\{-1,1\}^n$, possibly depending on $J$, and let $Z$ be an independent standard Gaussian vector. Set $Y=TX+\sqrt T\,Z$. Then, conditionally on $J$, except on an event of probability at most $e^{-cn}$, the inequality $$\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{\mu_Y}(f)\le 2\mathcal D_Y(f) \label{eq:terminal-poincare}$$ holds simultaneously for every real function $f$ on the cube. All constants and matrix events are independent of the law of $X$.
  - Explanation: After a sufficiently large fixed observation time, the posterior Gibbs law satisfies the Poincaré inequality for all functions outside an event of exponentially small probability, for any initial spin law.

Simultaneously for all words $Q$ of length at most $L$, all their real diagonal factors of norm at most $M_0$, and all $0\\le A\\le a_+I$, one has $$\\label{eq:word-estimates}\n \\lVert Q\\rVert\\le C,\\qquad\n \\left(\\sum_{a\\ne b}|Q_{ab}|^4\\right)^{1/4}\\le C,\\qquad\n \\lVert \\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(Q-\\mathsf P(Q))\\rVert_2\\le C.$$ For a word containing $K_A(J)$, the assertion is restricted to parameters satisfying $S_A(1;J)\\succeq cI$. The same conclusion holds with $W$ in place of $J$, with the corresponding stability restriction. Thus all diagonal parameters in this assertion may be chosen after the random matrix has been observed.*\n\n*If a word has $r$ ordinary occurrences of $M$ outside its one $K_A(z;M)$ factor, then $\\mathsf P(Q;z)$ has degree at most $r$.",

```json
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-matrix-path-stability",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $j,a_+$, with the following property. For every fixed deterministic $0\\le A\\le a_+I$, and every fixed $R>2\\sqrt j$, there are constants $C,c_2>0$ and $n_0$, uniform in $A$, such that $$\\label{eq:deterministic-path-stability}\n \\mathbb P\\left\\{\\lVert W\\rVert\\le R\\ \\hbox{and}\\quad\n S_A(z;W)\\succeq c_0I\\text{ for all }0\\le z\\le1\\right\\}\n \\ge1-Ce^{-c_2n}\\qquad(n\\ge n_0).$$ In addition, for every fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, $\\mathbb P\\{\\lVert W\\rVert>2\\sqrt j+\\varepsilon\\}\\le\nC_\\varepsilon e^{-c_\\varepsilon n}$ for sufficiently large $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-matrix-path-stability",
          "exact_quote": "There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $j,a_+$, with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-227.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bilinear",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $m\\ge1$, a vector bound $M_1<\\infty$, a tolerance $\\varepsilon>0$, and $R>2\\sqrt j$. For every deterministic $0\\le A\\le a_+I$ and deterministic vectors $v_1,\\ldots,v_m\\in\\mathbb R^n$ with $\\lVert v_r\\rVert\\le M_1$, there is an event of probability at least $1-Ce^{-c_3n}$ on which <a href=\"#eq:deterministic-path-stability\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:deterministic-path-stability\">[eq:deterministic-path-stability]</a> holds and, with $$G_A=A^{1/2}S_A(1;W)^{-1}A^{1/2}=K_A(W)A,$$ one has, for every $r,s$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |v_r^T(G_A-A)v_s|&\\le\\varepsilon,\n \\label{eq:bilinear-G}\\\\\n |v_r^T(G_AW-B_AA)v_s|&\\le\\varepsilon,\n \\label{eq:bilinear-GW}\\\\\n |v_r^T(WG_AW-B_AI-B_A^2A)v_s|&\\le\\varepsilon.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-WGW}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C,c_3,n_0$ may depend on $j,a_+,m,M_1,\\varepsilon,R$ but are uniform in $A$, the vectors, and $n\\ge n_0$. The assertion is for each deterministic choice of these parameters; it does not claim simultaneity over all vector choices.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bilinear",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-logdet",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\varepsilon>0$, an integer $r_0\\ge0$, and $M_2<\\infty$. There is $\\gamma_0>0$, depending only on $j,\\varepsilon$, such that for every fixed $0<\\gamma\\le\\gamma_0$ and all sufficiently large $n$, an event of probability at least $1-Ce^{-c_4n}$ has the following property. Simultaneously for all diagonal $0\\le V\\le I$ and all real matrices $E$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}E\\le r_0$ and $\\lVert E\\rVert\\le M_2$, put $$b=\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}V,\\qquad B=jb,\\qquad\n L=I+(BI-W)V+E.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:logdet-upper-bound}\n \\frac1n\\log\\det(L^TL+\\gamma I)\\le jb^2+\\varepsilon.$$ The constants $C,c_4$ and the lower threshold on $n$ may depend on $j,\\varepsilon,\\gamma,r_0,M_2$. In particular, $V$ and $E$ may depend on $W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-entropy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<j<1$, $d\\in\\mathbb R$, and $s>0$.         "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-equality",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<j<1$. For each $t\\ge0$, the equation $$s_*(t)=t+j\\mathbb E_{N(s_*(t),s_*(t))}\\tanh y\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point}$$ has a unique nonnegative solution, where $N(0,0)$ means the point mass at zero. Its associated Gaussian law $P_*(t)=N(s_*(t),s_*(t))$ is the unique Gaussian law satisfying <a href=\"#eq:scalar-consistency\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-consistency\">[eq:scalar-consistency]</a> with $\\sigma=0$. Thus, among consistent laws with $s>0$, equality in <a href=\"#eq:scalar-entropy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-entropy\">[eq:scalar-entropy]</a> holds precisely at $P_*(t)$.*\n\n*The function $t\\mapsto s_*(t)$ is Lipschitz with constant $(1-j)^{-1}$, and $$t\\le s_*(t)\\le\\frac{t}{1-j},\\qquad\n q_*(t):=\\mathbb E_{P_*(t)}\\tanh^2 y\n       =\\frac{s_*(t)-t}{j}\\le\\frac{t}{1-j}.\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point-bounds}$$ In particular $s_*(0)=q_*(0)=0$, and $s_*(t),q_*(t)>0$ for $t>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-equality",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<j<1$. For each $t\\ge0$, the equation $$s_*(t)=t+j\\mathbb E_{N(s_*(t),s_*(t))}\\tanh y\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point}$$ has a unique nonnegative solution, where $N(0,0)$ means the point mass at zero. Its associated Gaussian law $P_*(t)=N(s_*(t),s_*(t))$ is the unique Gaussian law satisfying <a href=\"#eq:scalar-consistency\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-consistency\">[eq:scalar-consistency]</a> with $\\sigma=0$. Thus, among consistent laws with $s>0$, equality in <a href=\"#eq:scalar-entropy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-entropy\">[eq:scalar-entropy]</a> holds precisely at $P_*(t)$.*\n\n*The function $t\\mapsto s_*(t)$ is Lipschitz with constant $(1-j)^{-1}$, and $$t\\le s_*(t)\\le\\frac{t}{1-j},\\qquad\n q_*(t):=\\mathbb E_{P_*(t)}\\tanh^2 y\n       =\\frac{s_*(t)-t}{j}\\le\\frac{t}{1-j}.\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point-bounds}$$ In particular $s_*(0)=q_*(0)=0$, and $s_*(t),q_*(t)>0$ for $t>0$.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<j<1$. For each $t\\ge0$, the equation $$s_*(t)=t+j\\mathbb E_{N(s_*(t),s_*(t))}\\tanh y\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point}$$ has a unique nonnegative solution, where $N(0,0)$ means the point mass at zero. Its associated Gaussian law $P_*(t)=N(s_*(t),s_*(t))$ is the unique Gaussian law satisfying <a href=\"#eq:scalar-consistency\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-consistency\">[eq:scalar-consistency]</a> with $\\sigma=0$. Thus, among consistent laws with $s>0$, equality in <a href=\"#eq:scalar-entropy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-entropy\">[eq:scalar-entropy]</a> holds precisely at $P_*(t)$.*\n\n*The function $t\\mapsto s_*(t)$ is Lipschitz with constant $(1-j)^{-1}$, and $$t\\le s_*(t)\\le\\frac{t}{1-j},\\qquad\n q_*(t):=\\mathbb E_{P_*(t)}\\tanh^2 y\n       =\\frac{s_*(t)-t}{j}\\le\\frac{t}{1-j}.\n \\label{eq:scalar-fixed-point-bounds}$$ In particular $s_*(0)=q_*(0)=0$, and $s_*(t),q_*(t)>0$ for $t>0$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-stable-fields",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $j\\in(0,1)$ and $0<T<\\infty$. There are constants $\\eta,c,\\epsilon_A,\\rho_0,a>0$, depending only on $j,T$, and events $\\mathcal J_n$ for the zero-diagonal Gaussian matrix $J$, such that $\\mathbb P(J\\in\\mathcal J_n)\\longrightarrow1$ and the following assertion holds for every $J\\in\\mathcal J_n$. Let $X\\sim\\mu_0$, let $B_0$ be an independent standard $n$-dimensional Brownian motion, and put $Y(t)=tX+B_0(t)$. With conditional probability at least $1-e^{-an}$, simultaneously for $0\\le t\\le T$, $y\\in\\mathbb R^n$, and all diagonal $A$, $$\\left.\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\lVert F_{J,Y(t)}(y)\\rVert\\le2\\rho_0\\sqrt n,\\\\\n 0\\le A\\le(1+\\eta)I,\\qquad\n \\lVert A-V_y\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\\le\\epsilon_A\\sqrt n\n \\end{gathered}\n \\right\\}\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\mathsf H_J(y,A)\\succ cI.\n \\label{eq:stable-field}$$ We may require $j(1+\\eta)^2<1$ and $\\lVert J\\rVert\\le2\\sqrt j+\\theta$ on $\\mathcal J_n$, for any sufficiently small fixed $\\theta>0$ chosen in the proof.",
        "source_id": "PM-227.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-rates",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $j\\in(0,1)$ and $T<\\infty$.*\n\n1.  *For every $s_{\\min}>0$ and $L<\\infty$, one can choose $R<\\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, uniformly over $0\\le t\\le T$ and $0<\\sigma\\le1$, $$\\int_{\\{s\\ge s_{\\min},\\,\\langle y^2\\rangle>R^2\\}}\n     G_{t,\\sigma,n}(y)\\,\\mathrm dy\\le e^{-Ln}.$$ For every $\\varepsilon>0$, the unrestricted integral over $\\{s\\ge s_{\\min}\\}$ is at most $e^{\\varepsilon n}$ for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n2.  *Fix $t_0>0$. Outside any prescribed open neighborhood of the equality curve $\\{(P_t,t):t_0\\le t\\le T\\}$, there are $a,\\sigma_*>0$ such that the integral of $G_{t,\\sigma,n}$ is at most $e^{-an}$, uniformly over $t_0\\le t\\le T$ and $0<\\sigma\\le\\sigma_*$, for all sufficiently large $n$. The neighborhood can impose weak closeness of the empirical law and closeness of any finite list of continuous, at most linearly growing averages, including all parameters in <a href=\"#eq:scalar-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scalar-parameters\">[eq:scalar-parameters]</a>.*\n\n3.  *For every $q_0>0$, there are $t_0,\\sigma_*,a>0$ such that $$\\int_{\\{q\\ge q_0\\}}G_{t,\\sigma,n}(y)\\,\\mathrm dy\\le e^{-an}$$ uniformly over $0\\le t\\le t_0$ and $0<\\sigma\\le\\sigma_*$, for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n4.  *Fix $t_0>0$ and $\\nu>0$. For all sufficiently large $R_1$ there is $a>0$ such that $$\\int_{\\{\\langle y^2\\mathbf 1_{\\{|y|>R_1\\}}\\rangle>\\nu\\}}\n     G_{t,\\sigma,n}(y)\\,\\mathrm dy\\le e^{-an},$$ uniformly over $t_0\\le t\\le T$ and $0<\\sigma\\le1$, for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n*All constants are independent of $n,t,\\sigma$ in the indicated ranges. In <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, neighborhoods are interpreted on each fixed second-moment ball; the complement of a sufficiently large such ball is covered by <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<t_0\\le T$, $R<\\infty$, and $\\eta>0$ with $j(1+\\eta)^2<1$. There are $c_*,\\epsilon_*,a_*,\\sigma_*>0$, a neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of $\\{(P_t,t):t_0\\le t\\le T\\}$, a tail tolerance $\\nu>0$, and a finite $R_1$ such that the following holds. Suppose $$\\langle y^2\\rangle\\le R^2,\\qquad (P_y,t)\\in\\mathcal U,\\qquad\n \\langle y^2\\mathbf 1_{\\{|y|>R_1\\}}\\rangle\\le\\nu,\n \\qquad t_0\\le t\\le T,\\quad0<\\sigma\\le\\sigma_*.$$ Under the Gaussian conditioning in <a href=\"#eq:conditional-integral\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:conditional-integral\">[eq:conditional-integral]</a>, with probability at least $1-e^{-a_*n}$, simultaneously for all diagonal $A$ satisfying $$0\\le A\\le(1+\\eta)I,\\qquad\n \\lVert A-V_y\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\\le\\epsilon_*\\sqrt n,$$ one has $\\mathsf H_{\\widetilde J}(y,A)\\succeq c_*I$. All constants are uniform over the displayed ranges and sufficiently large $n$. The tail cutoff $R_1$ may be required to be arbitrarily large after choosing $\\nu$; $\\epsilon_*$ is then decreased if necessary.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $j,K<\\infty$, $r>0$, and $\\varepsilon>0$. Suppose $\\lVert \\widetilde J\\rVert\\le K$, and a set $E\\subseteq\\mathbb R^n$ contains the ball $\\{y':\\lVert y'-y\\rVert\\le r\\sqrt n\\}$. Put $$L=F'_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y)\n =I+(BI-\\widetilde J)V_y-\\frac{2j}{n}mm^{\\mathsf T}V_y,\\qquad\n Q=L^{\\mathsf T}L+\\gamma I.$$ If $$\\frac1n\\log\\det Q\\le jb(y)^2+\\varepsilon_1,\\qquad\n \\lVert F_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y)\\rVert\\le\\rho\\sqrt n,\n \\label{eq:local-volume-hypotheses}$$ then $$(2\\pi\\sigma^2)^{-n/2}\\int_E\n e^{-\\lVert F_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y')\\rVert^2/(2\\sigma^2)+njb(y')^2/2}\\,\\mathrm dy'\n \\ge e^{-\\varepsilon n}\n \\label{eq:local-volume-conclusion}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, provided the constants are chosen in this order: first $\\varepsilon_1>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $\\varepsilon$; then any fixed $\\gamma>0$; then $\\sigma>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $j,K,r,\\varepsilon,\\gamma$; and finally $\\rho>0$ sufficiently small in terms of those constants and $\\sigma$. The bound is uniform in $y,h_0,n$ under the stated hypotheses.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-field-volume",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $j,K<\\infty$, $r>0$, and $\\varepsilon>0$. Suppose $\\lVert \\widetilde J\\rVert\\le K$, and a set $E\\subseteq\\mathbb R^n$ contains the ball $\\{y':\\lVert y'-y\\rVert\\le r\\sqrt n\\}$. Put $$L=F'_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y)\n =I+(BI-\\widetilde J)V_y-\\frac{2j}{n}mm^{\\mathsf T}V_y,\\qquad\n Q=L^{\\mathsf T}L+\\gamma I.$$ If $$\\frac1n\\log\\det Q\\le jb(y)^2+\\varepsilon_1,\\qquad\n \\lVert F_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y)\\rVert\\le\\rho\\sqrt n,\n \\label{eq:local-volume-hypotheses}$$ then $$(2\\pi\\sigma^2)^{-n/2}\\int_E\n e^{-\\lVert F_{\\widetilde J,h_0}(y')\\rVert^2/(2\\sigma^2)+njb(y')^2/2}\\,\\mathrm dy'\n \\ge e^{-\\varepsilon n}\n \\label{eq:local-volume-conclusion}$$ for all sufficiently large $n$, provided the constants are chosen in this order: first $\\varepsilon_1>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $\\varepsilon$; then any fixed $\\gamma>0$; then $\\sigma>0$ sufficiently small in terms of $j,K,r,\\varepsilon,\\gamma$; and finally $\\rho>0$ sufficiently small in terms of those constants and $\\sigma$. The bound is uniform in $y,h_0,n$ under the stated hypotheses.",
          "source_id": "PM-227.3"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $j,K,c,r_0>0$. Let $J$ be symmetric with $\\lVert J\\rVert\\le K$, and let $h_0\\in\\mathbb R^n$. Suppose $$\\mathsf H_J(y,V_y)\\succeq cI\n \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad\n \\lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\\rVert\\le r_0\\sqrt n.\n \\label{eq:root-stability-assumption}$$ Then $F_{J,h_0}$ has exactly one zero $r\\in\\mathbb R^n$, and $$\\lVert y-r\\rVert\\le C\\lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\\rVert\n \\quad\\text{if}\\quad\\lVert F_{J,h_0}(y)\\rVert<r_0\\sqrt n,\n \\qquad C=1+(K+3j)/c.\n \\label{eq:root-distance}$$ In particular this conclusion applies to every field satisfying <a href=\"#eq:stable-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stable-field\">[eq:stable-field]</a>, even if that implication is required only through residual threshold $\\rho_0\\sqrt n$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-primary-derivatives",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$H_1=J,\\qquad M_0=I,\\qquad\n M_l=D_{1-(m^l)^2}H_l,\\qquad\n H_{l+1}=JM_l-jb_lM_{l-1}.\n \\label{eq:primary-formal}$$ For each fixed $l\\geq1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\lVert \\nabla h^l-H_l\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\n   +\\lVert \\nabla m^l-M_l\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\\leq C,\\label{eq:primary-error}\\\\\n &\\lVert \\nabla h^l\\rVert+\\lVert \\nabla m^l\\rVert\n   +\\lVert \\nabla h^l\\rVert_{\\ell^4}\n   +\\lVert \\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}\\nabla h^l\\rVert_2\\leq C,\n   \\qquad \\lVert db_l\\rVert\\leq Cn^{-1/2}.\n \\label{eq:primary-bounds}\n\\end{aligned}$$ More generally, let $g_i$ be a smooth function of a fixed list of coordinates $h_i^l$ and averages $b_l$, with uniformly bounded derivatives through the orders being used. Deterministic site labels are permitted, provided the same bounds hold uniformly in $i$. Its derivative matrix has bounded operator norm, bounded diagonal $\\ell^2$ norm, and bounded entrywise $\\ell^4$ norm.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-implicit-construction",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small $\\rho>0$, <a href=\"#eq:implicit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:implicit\">[eq:implicit]</a> has a unique solution on $\\Omega_{2\\rho}$. There are constants independent of the primary depth $k$ such that, throughout this region, $$\\lVert w\\rVert+\\lVert y^{\\rm imp}\\rVert+\\sqrt n|c_1|\\leq C,\n \\qquad\n I+j\\chi A-A^{1/2}JA^{1/2}\\succeq cI.\n \\label{eq:implicit-size}$$ On $\\Omega_{3\\rho/2}$, for all sufficiently large $n$ depending on the fixed depth, the solution also satisfies $$\\lVert \\nabla w\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}+\\lVert \\nabla y^{\\rm imp}\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\n       +\\sqrt n\\lVert dc_1\\rVert\\leq C_k.\n \\label{eq:implicit-frobenius}$$",

        "exact_quote": "Every source, auxiliary field, and terminal vector $U$ in a fixed ordinary admissible recipe satisfies $$\\lVert U\\rVert+\\lVert \\nabla U\\rVert_{\\mathrm F}\n                   +\\lVert \\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}\\nabla U\\rVert_1\\leq C.\n \\label{eq:small-derivative-bound}$$ The same holds locally for recipes with the single initial implicit field above.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-residual",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a terminal vector in a fixed recipe admissible from level $p\\geq1$. For an ordinary recipe and every scalar $G$, $$\\left|\\mathbb EG R_p^{\\mathsf T}U\\right|\\leq C\\lVert G\\rVert_*.\n \\label{eq:weak-residual}$$ For a local recipe with implicit starting index $k$, the same conclusion holds when $p\\leq k$ and $G$ is supported on $\\Omega_\\rho$.*\n\n\n*There is also an ordinary square-density version. If $\\sup_x(|H_0(x)|+\\lVert dH_0(x)\\rVert)\\leq C_0$, then for every $f$, $$\\left|\\mathbb Ef^2H_0 R_p^{\\mathsf T}U\\right|\n       \\leq C\\{\\mathbb Ef^2+\\mathcal D_{h_0}(f)\\}.\n \\label{eq:square-residual}$$ Constants are uniform in $f,G,H_0$ subject to the stated bound, and may depend on $p$, the recipe, its coefficient bounds, and the finite set of word diagnostics needed in the proof.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-residual-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For each sufficiently small fixed $\\rho>0$, there is a fixed depth $d$ and functions $C_k$, $2\\le k\\le d$, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$0\\le C_k\\le1$, and $C_k$ is supported where $\\max(\\lVert R_k\\rVert,\\lVert R_{k-1}\\rVert)\\le\\rho\\sqrt n$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Ordinary admissible recipe** ([source](#stmt-def-small-recipe)): Choose finitely many deterministic seeds $s\in\mathbb R^n$ of bounded Euclidean norm. Seeds may depend on $J$ and $h_0$, but are fixed as functions of $x$. Choose a finite tuple of scalar functions $\theta$ satisfying $$\sup_x\bigl(\|\theta_\alpha(x)\|+\lVert d\theta_\alpha(x)\rVert\bigr)\leq C$$ for each component. Such scalar functions will be called parameters; they may include the averages $b_l$. Given auxiliary fields $y^1,\ldots,y^{a-1}$ already constructed, a source has the form $$w_i^a=\sum_{s}\phi_{a,s,i}\bigl((h_i^l)_{l\in I_a},\theta\bigr)s_i +\sum_{b<a}\phi_{a,b,i}\bigl((h_i^l)_{l\in I_a},\theta\bigr)y_i^b. \label{eq:source-form}$$ All sums and index sets are finite. Coefficients are smooth and bounded, with bounded derivatives of every fixed order needed in the construction, uniformly on their argument ranges and the connecting line segments. Define the new auxiliary field by $$y^a=Jw^a -j\sum_l\langle \partial_lw^a\rangle\,m^{l-1} -j\sum_{b<a}\langle \partial_{y^b}w^a\rangle\,w^b. \label{eq:small-field}$$ The partial $\partial_l$ differentiates the explicitly displayed local argument $h_i^l$, holding every parameter and every auxiliary argument fixed. In particular it holds $b_l$ fixed even when that average is among the parameters. The partial $\partial_{y^b}$ differentiates the indicated linear auxiliary argument, with its coefficient held fixed. A partial in an absent argument is zero. Thus $\partial_{y^b}w^a$ is simply the coefficient vector $\phi_{a,b}$. A terminal vector is another source of the form <a href="#eq:source-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-form">[eq:source-form]</a>, using any already constructed auxiliary fields. A source $w^b$ used again as a terminal vector or in a new source is replaced by its expression <a href="#eq:source-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-form">[eq:source-form]</a>; auxiliary fields themselves are not expanded. The recipe is *admissible from level $p$* if every explicit primary argument appearing in its source formulas or terminal formula has index at least $p$. This condition does not restrict the parameters, or the vectors $m^{l-1}$ in the correction terms of <a href="#eq:small-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-field">[eq:small-field]</a>.
- **Centered GOE matrix** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A centered Gaussian orthogonal ensemble (GOE) matrix of scale $j$ has variances $j/n$ off diagonal and $2j/n$ on the diagonal.
- **Word** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *word* is an ordered product of diagonal matrices, copies of a symmetric matrix $M$, and at most one factor $K_A(M)$.
- **Relative entropy** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For probability laws $\nu,\mu$, their relative entropy is $D(\nu\Vert\mu)=\int\log(\mathrm d\nu/\mathrm d\mu)\,\mathrm d\nu$ when $\nu\ll\mu$, and $+\infty$ otherwise.

```json
      "exact_definition": "Choose finitely many deterministic seeds $s\\in\\mathbb R^n$ of bounded Euclidean norm. Seeds may depend on $J$ and $h_0$, but are fixed as functions of $x$. Choose a finite tuple of scalar functions $\\theta$ satisfying $$\\sup_x\\bigl(|\\theta_\\alpha(x)|+\\lVert d\\theta_\\alpha(x)\\rVert\\bigr)\\leq C$$ for each component. Such scalar functions will be called parameters; they may include the averages $b_l$.\n\n\n\nGiven auxiliary fields $y^1,\\ldots,y^{a-1}$ already constructed, a source has the form $$w_i^a=\\sum_{s}\\phi_{a,s,i}\\bigl((h_i^l)_{l\\in I_a},\\theta\\bigr)s_i\n       +\\sum_{b<a}\\phi_{a,b,i}\\bigl((h_i^l)_{l\\in I_a},\\theta\\bigr)y_i^b.\n \\label{eq:source-form}$$ All sums and index sets are finite. Coefficients are smooth and bounded, with bounded derivatives of every fixed order needed in the construction, uniformly on their argument ranges and the connecting line segments. Define the new auxiliary field by $$y^a=Jw^a\n       -j\\sum_l\\langle \\partial_lw^a\\rangle\\,m^{l-1}\n       -j\\sum_{b<a}\\langle \\partial_{y^b}w^a\\rangle\\,w^b.\n \\label{eq:small-field}$$ The partial $\\partial_l$ differentiates the explicitly displayed local argument $h_i^l$, holding every parameter and every auxiliary argument fixed. In particular it holds $b_l$ fixed even when that average is among the parameters. The partial $\\partial_{y^b}$ differentiates the indicated linear auxiliary argument, with its coefficient held fixed. A partial in an absent argument is zero. Thus $\\partial_{y^b}w^a$ is simply the coefficient vector $\\phi_{a,b}$.\n\nA terminal vector is another source of the form <a href=\"#eq:source-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-form\">[eq:source-form]</a>, using any already constructed auxiliary fields. A source $w^b$ used again as a terminal vector or in a new source is replaced by its expression <a href=\"#eq:source-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:source-form\">[eq:source-form]</a>; auxiliary fields themselves are not expanded. The recipe is *admissible from level $p$* if every explicit primary argument appearing in its source formulas or terminal formula has index at least $p$. This condition does not restrict the parameters, or the vectors $m^{l-1}$ in the correction terms of <a href=\"#eq:small-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-field\">[eq:small-field]</a>.",
      "interpretation": "A finite construction of test vectors from bounded seeds, bounded parameters and auxiliary fields with Onsager-type corrections.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Ordinary admissible recipe",
      "scope": "Residual and derivative estimates for the posterior comparison."
    },
```

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-discrete-gap | spectral gap of the uniform-site discrete heat-bath chain | ≥ | $1/(C_\beta n)$ | fixed inverse temperature below one |
| q-poincare-constant | Poincaré constant | = | $4e^{C_{\mathrm d}T}$ | fixed terminal time and directional constant |
| q-bad-path-probability | probability of a bad stopped path | ≤ | $2e^{-a n}$ | fixed rate after decreasing it if necessary |
| q-weighted-bad-probability | probability of bad paths under the reweighted law | ≤ | $v/4$ | large dimension, tolerance chosen once |
| q-stopped-variance-lower | expected stopped posterior variance | ≥ | $v-(1-v)d_0$ | directional constant fixed |
| q-dirichlet-lower | initial Dirichlet form of a normalized function | ≥ | $\frac v4$ | fixed constants chosen before the dimension grows |
| q-terminal-poincare | variance of the posterior law | ≤ | $2\mathcal D_Y(f)$ | every real function on the cube, outside an exponentially rare event |
| q-gaussian-lipschitz | Lipschitz constant of the consistent variance in time | = | $(1-j)^{-1}$ | time nonnegative |
| q-root-inverse-constant | distance-to-root constant | = | $1+(K+3j)/c$ | residual below the threshold |
| q-stopping-probability | probability that the stopping time falls before the horizon | ≤ | $e^{-an}$ | fixed horizon |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-path-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-matrix-path-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-path-stability): Gaussian comparison for the quadratic process, concentration, and a finite mesh along the path. -> positive margin along the interpolation path
- **s-word-bias** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-bias) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-bias): Gaussian integration by parts with truncation, loop equations and induction on the number of ordinary letters. -> expected diagonal equals the noncrossing prediction up to a small error
- **s-words** [claim](#stmt-lem-words) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-bias-2): Concentration with dyadic meshes over the parameter box, plus sign symmetry and the expectation estimate. -> simultaneous word estimates
- **s-bilinear** [claim](#stmt-lem-bilinear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bilinear): Expectation of each bilinear form followed by Gaussian concentration. -> deterministic equivalents for bilinear forms
- **s-logdet** [claim](#stmt-lem-logdet) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-logdet): Interpolation for the mean, regularization for a Lipschitz bound, a finite mesh over diagonals and a finite-rank perturbation argument. -> uniform regularized determinant bound
- **s-scalar-entropy** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-entropy): Monotone change of variables with a concavity estimate, then a second-moment Gaussian comparison. -> entropy lower bound with equality characterization
- **s-gaussian-equality** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-equality): Gaussian symmetry identities and a contraction argument for the fixed point. -> unique consistent Gaussian law
- **s-gaussian-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-reduction): Gaussian covariance computation and conditioning of a jointly Gaussian pair. -> conditional reduction identity
- **s-scalar-rates** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-rates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-rates): Tail envelope, compactness with finite covers and variational entropy tests, and exponential tilting. -> uniform exponential rate bounds for the scalar integral
- **s-conditional-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-field-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-field-stability): Gaussian regression and whitening, an exact rank-three correction at the equality law, then perturbation and a counting argument over diagonals. -> positive conditional Hessian margin near the equality curve
- **s-local-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-field-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-field-volume): Integrate over a Gaussian ellipsoid at the approximate zero, control Taylor remainders and apply Jensen's inequality. -> lower bound on the field integral over a ball
- **s-stable-fields** [claim](#stmt-prop-stable-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-field-volume-2): Planted integral estimate, exclusion of unstable approximate zeros by local volume, diagonal removal, a mesh in time and size-bias undoing. -> stability along observation paths
- **s-stopping** [claim](#stmt-cor-stopping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-stopping): Define a hitting time of the least residual among unstable witnesses and apply the stability proposition. -> test-function-independent stopping time
- **s-root** [claim](#stmt-lem-root) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root): Inverse bound from the Hessian proxy, minimization of a potential on a box with gradient-flow uniqueness, and an ordinary differential equation path. -> unique root and quantitative inversion
- **s-primary-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-primary-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primary-derivatives): Bound formal matrix chains with word estimates, then compare with discrete derivatives by Taylor's theorem. -> derivative bounds for primary iterates
- **s-implicit** [claim](#stmt-lem-implicit-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-implicit-construction): Solve the linear implicit system with the stable inverse margin, then compare equations before and after one spin flip. -> implicit field with size and derivative bounds
- **s-small-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-derivatives): Bound source errors, then cancel formal traces along dependency paths by inclusion–exclusion over adjacent pairs. -> size, Frobenius and diagonal derivative bounds for small vectors
- **s-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residual): Conditional integration by parts at the first level and strong induction using the scalar tangent-difference identity. -> weak residual rule
- **s-terminal-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-residual): Rewrite the pairing with the implicit source through earlier residuals and apply the weak rule. -> terminal residual identity
- **s-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-residual-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residual-selection): Telescoping squared increments, moment bounds from the weak rule, smooth cutoffs and a union bound. -> small-residual regions with small deficit probability
- **s-directional** [claim](#stmt-prop-directional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-directional): Test the implicit field along unit directions on each selected region and control the scalar coefficient error by absorption. -> directional covariance bound
- **s-weighted-mean** [claim](#stmt-prop-weighted-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-weighted-mean): Square-density weak residual rule with cutoffs and comparison to the root on each cutoff support. -> weighted-mean estimate
- **s-curvature** [claim](#stmt-lem-curvature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curvature): Exact two-spin conditional calculation with Young's inequality, summed over pairs. -> signed curvature inequality
- **s-terminal-matrices** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-matrices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-matrices): Quadratic-form tails, sphere nets, row tails and union bounds over small coordinate sets. -> global and small-submatrix bounds
- **s-terminal-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-gap): Count coordinates with small observation field, split the quadratic form on the small set and its complement, then convert curvature into a gap. -> terminal Poincaré inequality
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Variance retention along the path, entropy bound for bad paths under reweighting, and the terminal gap on good paths. -> dimension-free Poincaré inequality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-dimension-free-poincare**: For each fixed inverse temperature below one, there is a constant depending only on that temperature such that, with probability tending to one over the disorder, the variance of every real function under the zero-field Gibbs law is at most that constant times the unscaled heat-bath Dirichlet form.
- **inv-discrete-gap-scaling**: The discrete-time chain that picks a uniform site has spectral gap at least the reciprocal of the constant times the dimension, with probability tending to one over the disorder.
- **inv-stable-field-observation**: Along the Gaussian observation path, with high conditional probability and on a typical disorder event, every small-residual field with a diagonal close to the conditional-variance diagonal has a Hessian proxy bounded below by a positive constant.
- **inv-unique-root**: If the Hessian proxy is bounded below throughout the small-residual region, the field equation has exactly one zero, and the distance to it is bounded linearly by the residual.
- **inv-terminal-gap**: After a sufficiently large fixed observation time, outside an exponentially small event, the posterior Gibbs law satisfies a Poincare inequality with constant two, for any initial spin law.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-temperature**: Quoting the dimension-free gap for temperatures at or above the critical value. -> The statement is only proved for a fixed inverse temperature strictly below one; nothing is claimed beyond it.
- **anti-non-gaussian-disorder**: Transferring the gap to sparse, structured or deterministic coupling matrices. -> The proof uses Gaussian concentration, comparison and integration by parts for dense Gaussian disorder; these tools are unavailable for other couplings.
- **anti-worst-case-guarantee**: Treating the gap as a guarantee for every single coupling matrix at a given size. -> The statement holds only with probability tending to one over the disorder, so particular matrices may fall outside the good event.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result and its supporting lemmas are asymptotic, probabilistic existence statements about random Gaussian matrices and Gibbs measures; the constants are not given by a finite procedure and the events hold only with probability tending to one, so there is no finite constructive object or algorithm to run and check against an independent oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-subcritical-temperature** (hypothesis): The gap inequality is proved only for a fixed inverse temperature strictly below one, chosen before the dimension grows. The constant may depend on that choice. Nothing is asserted at or above the critical value. Blocked use: ['Quoting a dimension-free gap for temperatures at or above one', 'Letting the inverse temperature approach one as the dimension grows while keeping the same constant']
- **L-probabilistic-over-disorder** (edge_case): The conclusion holds on an event of disorder probability tending to one. It is not a guarantee for every coupling matrix of a given size, and no rate for the exceptional event is stated in the theorem. Blocked use: ['Certifying the gap for one specific coupling matrix as a worst-case guarantee', 'Treating the discrete-chain gap as a deterministic bound at a finite size']
- **L-nonexplicit-constant** (unknown): The Poincaré constant is expressed through constants fixed during the proof and is not optimized. No numerical value for a given temperature is provided. Blocked use: ['Using the constant to size a number of sampler steps in practice', 'Claiming a sharp or optimal dependence of the gap on temperature']
- **L-gaussian-disorder-ensemble** (formal_scope): The matrix estimates rely on dense Gaussian couplings with zero diagonal and symmetric structure, via Gaussian concentration and integration by parts. The estimates are not claimed for other coupling ensembles. Blocked use: ['Applying the result to sparse, structured or deterministic coupling matrices', 'Applying it to non-Gaussian disorder without re-deriving the word estimates']
- **L-stable-field-regime** (hypothesis): The stability along observation paths is proved for a fixed subcritical coupling scale and a fixed finite observation horizon, with constants depending only on these. It is restricted to a typical disorder event. Blocked use: ['Extending the stable-field implication to unbounded observation time', 'Using it for a coupling scale at or above the critical value']
- **L-root-needs-whole-region** (hypothesis): Uniqueness of the mean-field root and the inverse bound require positivity of the Hessian proxy throughout the entire small-residual region, not only at one point. Blocked use: ['Concluding a unique root from positivity at a single approximate solution', 'Using the distance bound outside the residual threshold']
- **L-root-existence-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): Existence of the root comes from minimizing a potential on a box and a gradient-flow argument. No algorithm with a stated cost or convergence rate is given for finding the root. Blocked use: ['Presenting the proof as a root-finding procedure with a complexity bound', 'Assuming the finite recursion converges to the root']
- **L-word-estimates-adaptive-restriction** (formal_scope): The word estimates for words containing the inverse factor hold only at diagonal parameters meeting the stated stability margin. Bounded length and bounded diagonal norms are fixed in advance. Blocked use: ['Using the estimates for adaptive diagonals that violate the stability margin', 'Letting the word length grow with the dimension']
- **L-bilinear-not-simultaneous** (edge_case): The bilinear deterministic equivalents hold for each prescribed finite family of deterministic vectors, with exponentially small failure probability. No simultaneity over all vector choices is claimed. Blocked use: ['Applying the bilinear bounds to vectors chosen after observing the matrix', 'Taking a union over an unbounded family of vectors']
- **L-stopping-depends-on-disorder** (approximation): The stopping time depends on the coupling matrix and on the observations, but not on the test function. The exponentially small hitting probability holds only for matrices in the good event. Blocked use: ['Reusing the stopping rule as a quantity independent of the matrix', 'Applying the hitting-probability bound outside the good disorder event']
- **L-terminal-gap-large-n** (edge_case): The terminal gap is stated only for every sufficiently large dimension and for matrices in the good event. The exceptional event concerns the observation at that fixed matrix. Blocked use: ['Applying the terminal inequality at small dimensions without a separate argument', 'Reading the exceptional probability as averaged over the disorder']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 227 (`lean/docs/227.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A spectral gap throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/A-spectral-gap-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Cutoff throughout the high-temperature Sherrington–Kirkpatrick phase](../../preprints/Cutoff-throughout-the-high-temperature-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-phase-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Critical slowing down in the Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model](../../preprints/Critical-slowing-down-in-the-Sherrington-Kirkpatrick-model-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Stretched-exponential barriers for typical SK initial states](../../preprints/Stretched-exponential-barriers-for-typical-SK-initial-states-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> For every fixed inverse temperature $0<\beta<1$, the formalization proves a dimension-independent Poincaré inequality for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model with probability tending to one over the disorder. Equivalently, the unscaled single-site heat-bath spectral gap stays bounded away from zero. For dynamics that choose one site uniformly at each discrete step, the spectral gap is at least $1/(Cn)$ with probability tending to one, for a constant $C$ depending on $\beta$.
>
> The formalized supporting result proves ratio cutoff for discrete single-site heat-bath dynamics in the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model for $0\le\beta<1/2$. For every $0<\varepsilon<1/2$ and $\eta>0$, the probability over the disorder that $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(\varepsilon)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}(1-\varepsilon)>1+\eta$ tends to zero as $n\to\infty$. The denominator is positive for all sufficiently large sizes.
>
> The paper's full range $\beta<1$, explicit cutoff location, and continuous-time conclusion are outside this selected statement.
>
> At criticality in the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model, the formalization proves that for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, the continuous-time mixing time lies between $n^{2/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, and the discrete-time mixing count lies between $n^{5/3-\varepsilon}$ and $e^{\varepsilon n}$, with probability tending to one over the Gaussian disorder. The disorder has independent off-diagonal entries of variance $1/n$. Continuous updates have rate one per site; discrete time counts uniformly chosen single-site update attempts. Mixing is worst-start total variation at threshold $1/4$, with holding allowed.
>
> For deterministic times $t_n=o(n^{2/3})$ or update counts $k_n=o(n^{5/3})$, the Gibbs mass of realized starting states still farther than $1/4$ from equilibrium tends to one in probability. For every deterministic positive sequence $M_n\to\infty$, the covariance operator norm and the linear Rayleigh supremum, using the unscaled heat-bath Dirichlet form, both lie between $n^{2/3}/M_n$ and $M_n n^{2/3}$ with probability tending to one. The covariance result does not give an upper bound for the full inverse spectral gap.
>
> The formalization proves a stretched-exponential mixing obstruction for the zero-field Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model at every fixed inverse temperature $\beta>1$. At time $\exp(n^{1/10000})$, the expected Gibbs mass of initial configurations whose total-variation distance from equilibrium exceeds $1/4$ tends to one. Since this mass lies in $[0,1]$, it also tends to one in probability over the disorder.
>
> The result covers both rate-one-per-site continuous-time heat-bath dynamics and the same stated number of discrete update attempts.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators
open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter

namespace SKGap

abbrev Spin (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Bool
abbrev Edge (n : ℕ) := {p : Fin n × Fin n // p.1 < p.2}
abbrev Disorder (n : ℕ) := Edge n → ℝ

def spinValue (b : Bool) : ℝ := if b then 1 else -1

def coupling {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (i k : Fin n) : ℝ :=
  if h : i < k then g ⟨(i, k), h⟩ else
    if h : k < i then g ⟨(k, i), h⟩ else 0

def hamiltonian {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  (1 / 2 : ℝ) * ∑ i, ∑ k, spinValue (x i) * coupling g i k * spinValue (x k) +
    ∑ i, h i * spinValue (x i)

def weight {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  Real.exp (hamiltonian g h x)

def partition {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ x, weight g h x

def mass {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  weight g h x / partition g h

def expectation {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (f : Spin n → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ x, mass g h x * f x

def variance {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (f : Spin n → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  expectation g h (fun x => (f x - expectation g h f) ^ 2)

def flip {n : ℕ} (i : Fin n) (x : Spin n) : Spin n :=
  Function.update x i (!(x i))

/-- Conditional expectation under the actual Gibbs law given all other spins. -/
def conditionalExpectation {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ)
    (i : Fin n) (f : Spin n → ℝ) (x : Spin n) : ℝ :=
  (weight g h x * f x + weight g h (flip i x) * f (flip i x)) /
    (weight g h x + weight g h (flip i x))

/-- Unscaled form: each site has a rate-one clock. -/
def dirichlet {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) (h : Fin n → ℝ) (f : Spin n → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i, expectation g h (fun x => (f x - conditionalExpectation g h i f x) ^ 2)

def disorderLaw (β : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : Measure (Disorder n) :=
  Measure.pi (fun _ : Edge n => gaussianReal 0 (Real.toNNReal (β ^ 2 / n)))

def poincareEvent (n : ℕ) (C : ℝ) : Set (Disorder n) :=
  {g | ∀ f : Spin n → ℝ, variance g 0 f ≤ C * dirichlet g 0 f}

/-- Rayleigh quotient definition of the uniform-site discrete heat-bath gap. -/
def discreteGap {n : ℕ} (g : Disorder n) : ℝ :=
  sInf {r : ℝ | ∃ f : Spin n → ℝ, 0 < variance g 0 f ∧
    r = dirichlet g 0 f / ((n : ℝ) * variance g 0 f)}

def MainStatement (β : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => disorderLaw β n (poincareEvent n C)) atTop (nhds 1) ∧
    Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => disorderLaw β n {g | 1 / (C * (n : ℝ)) ≤ discreteGap g})
      atTop (nhds 1)

theorem sk_main (β : ℝ) (hβ0 : 0 < β) (hβ1 : β < 1) : MainStatement β := by
  sorry

end SKGap
end

end OAI
```

```json
  "toolchain": "leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1"
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.formal.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed inverse temperature $`0\lt \beta\lt 1`$, we prove that the unscaled spectral gap of single-site heat-bath dynamics for the zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick model is bounded away from zero with probability tending to one over the disorder. Equivalently, the Gibbs law satisfies a dimension-free Poincaré inequality for all functions.
